OIL FUTURES: Crude climbs as market weighs post-election outlook for US-Iran war
Democrats' win creates Iran policy uncertainty
Hormuz traffic rises despite tanker attacks
Crude oil futures settled higher Nov. 5 as markets clawed back some of the previous session's declines amid uncertain Middle East outlooks.
NYMEX December WTI settled $2.40/barrel higher at $90.52/b and ICE January Brent climbed $3.67/b to $98.29/b.
Analysts remain divided regarding the outlook for US-Iran tensions following a sweeping Democratic Party victory in the Nov. 3 midterm elections. While control of Congress empowers Democrats to limit funding and scrutinize White House actions, US President Donald Trump retains significant latitude in military decision-making, especially during the lame-duck session.
"How all this plays out is still highly uncertain. Therefore, the degree and speed to which the oil market has sold off seems to be overdone," wrote ING strategists Warren Patterson and Ewa Manthey. "One would think Trump might want to come away with some accomplishments from this war. Does he go about that through renewed strikes or striking a deal? Or by continuing to put pressure on Iran with the ongoing maritime blockade?"
NYMEX December RBOB rallied 3.48 cents/gallon to settle at $2.7634/gal and December ULSD climbed 14.64 cents/gal to $4.3519/gal.
The Democrats' midterm victory could increase the chance that Trump will turn his focus to foreign policy and potentially speed an escalation in the war with Iran, analysts told Platts, a part of S&P Global Energy.
(continued on page 39)

International Crude
CRUDE MOC: Middle East crude differentials edge higher
Platts Dubai, Oman premiums up 82 cents/b 14 Dubai partials trade during MOC

Cash differentials for Middle East sour crude markers edged higher during the Singapore Platts Market on Close assessment process Nov. 5.
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, assessed January cash Dubai and cash Oman both at a premium of $1.46/b to samemonth Dubai futures at the market close, up 82 cents/b on the day.
January cash Murban was up $3.86/b to a premium of $4.50/b to same-month Dubai futures.
During the MOC, 14 January Dubai partials of 25,000 barrels each traded. The sellers were Mercuria, TotalEnergies and BP, while the buyers were Shell, Vitol and Phillips 66.
No convergences were reached during the MOC. A convergence occurs when 20 partials are traded between two counterparties in the same direction, resulting in a full 500,000-barrel physical cargo being declared from the seller to the buyer.

Dubai crude futures volume on TOCOM rises 9.6% MOM to four-month high
Traded volume down 36.91% year over year
Dubai cash spread narrows amid weak fundamental

The volume of Dubai crude oil futures contracts traded on the Tokyo Commodity Exchange rose 9.6% month over month to 56,868 lots in October, marking a four-month high. Volume was last recorded higher at 62,210 lots in June 2026, the latest TOCOM data showed Nov. 5.
The figure was 36.9% lower than the traded volume reported in October 2025, according to the data.

Platts Oilgram Price Report

November 5, 2026

Each Dubai crude futures contract is 50,000 liters, equivalent to about 314.5 barrels.
The rise in volumes month over month came amid weaker fundamentals for the Middle East sour crude complex in October. Ample Middle East crude offers and sales were made by both producers and traders over the month, while refinery run cuts were heard taking place in parts of Asia as high oil prices and freight rates ate into domestic margins.
December cash Dubai assessments averaged $110.73/b in October, down from an average of $113.71/b for front-month November cash Dubai in September, while the Dubai cash-futures spread -- a measure of the market structure -- averaged $8.10/b in October, down from $21.60/b in September, data from Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, showed.
That weakness continued into the new January-loading trade cycle, with the Platts Dubai cash-futures spread falling to a threemonth-low premium of 30 cents/b on Nov. 2, the first trading day of the month. Trade sources noted an overhang of unsold cargoes from the prior cycle extending into the new month.

Indian ONGC's Q2 crude output down 4.2% on year to 4.44 mil mt
Quarterly crude price up 37%
Half-yearly crude output falls 4.5%

Indian state-owned upstream company Oil & Natural Gas Corp. posted a 4.2% year-on-year fall in crude output over JulySeptember from its own fields to 4.44 million mt, the company said Nov. 5.
ONGC's fiscal year runs from April to March. In the July-September period, ONGC's natural gas output fell 1.6% year over year to 4.84 Bcm, the company said. ONGC realized a price of $92.33/b in Q2 for crude produced from its own fields, up 37%, it said. In Q2, ONGC realized $7/MMBtu for gas produced from its own fields, up 3.7% year over year, it said. In Q2, the decline in crude and gas production was attributed mainly to reservoir complexities in KG-98/2 asset in the eastern offshore basin, operational challenges during the summer rain season and associated pipeline replacement issues in the monsoon season, the company said. Gas production from onshore fields was also affected by lower customer offtake during the quarter, following disruptions at certain major consumer facilities that temporarily reduced demand, ONGC said. ONGC has scaled up BP's engagement as Technical Service Provider (TSP) for the entire western offshore (TSP-1 & TSP-2), as

part of a strategy to enhance production from its mature fields through focused interventions to arrest natural decline and accelerate development activities, ONGC said.
Half-year For the April-September period or H1, ONGC posted a 4.5
% year-on-year fall in crude production to 8.89 million mt, while natural gas output fell 1.7% year-on-year to 9.59 Bcm, the company said.
ONGC realized a price of $95.84/b in H1 for crude produced from its own fields, up 43.6% year over year, it said.
In H1, ONGC realized $7/MMBtu for gas produced, up 4.6% year over year, it said.
In Q2, ONGC discovered two onshore oil and gas fields, the company said.
ONGC's crude oil output fell 1.2% year on year to 18.35 million mt in 2025-26 (April-March), while gas output fell 0.6% year on year to 19.53 Bcm in the last fiscal year, ONGC said.
ONGC shares around 56% in India's oil and gas output on a standalone basis excluding contributions from its joint ventures, according to the oil ministry.
The flagship state-run explorer has a mandate to raise India's oil and gas output to cut 85%-88% dependency on crude imports to meet the world's third-biggest importer and consumers' appetite for energy, the ministry said.
-- Ratnajyoti Dutta
Kazakhstan offering 112 exploration blocks via auctions in 2026
Production volatile amid infrastructure disruptions
Targeting output of 99 mil mt in 2027
Kazakhstan is offering 112 oil and gas blocks for exploration via electronic auctions in 2026, Energy Minister Yerlan Akkenzhenov said Nov. 4 during a government press briefing shown on the ministry's Instagram account.
Kazakhstan is aiming to boost investment in its oil production, which has been volatile in recent years. Output has been disrupted frequently due to attacks on key infrastructure, bad weather and maintenance.
Kazakhstan plans to produce 96 million metric tons of oil in 2026 and increase this to 99 million mt in 2027, Interfax reported Nov. 4, citing Akkenzhenov.
Kazakhstan produced 1.8 million barrels/day of crude in September, according to the Platts OPEC+ crude production survey by S&P Global Energy. Platts is part of S&P Global Energy.
Output has ranged from 1.3 million b/d in January to a 2026 high of 1.85 million b/d in April, according to the survey.
Between 2023 and 2025, Kazakhstan offered 126 exploration blocks through auctions involving domestic and foreign companies, Akkenzhenov said during the briefing broadcast on Instagram.

KazMunayGas CEO Askhat Khassenov said during the same briefing that the company's exploration program includes 47 exploration blocks across 23 projects. KMG estimates combined resources at 4.7 billion mt of oil equivalent, Khassenov said.
The company is also carrying out 60,000 linear kilometers of planned seismic surveying, with exploration reaching depths of up to 8,000 meters, he added.
-- Rosemary Griffin
ASIA CRUDE MOC PERFORMANCE: 3.5 million barrels change hands for October loading
Physical trade of seven Oman cargoes completed
Convergence cargo traded volumes down 78.6% YOY
Participants in the Platts Asia crude Market on Close assessment process have completed the physical performance of 3.5 million barrels of Oman crude for October loading, according to trade performance data compiled by Platts, part of S&P Global Energy.
Middle East crude cargoes trade on a two-month forward cycle, meaning that product loading in October resulted from convergence cargoes declared in August. A convergence occurs when 20 partials are traded between two counterparties, resulting in a full 500,000-barrel physical cargo being declared from the seller to the buyer.

A total of six convergence cargoes, equivalent to 3 million barrels of crude, were declared in August for loading in October, Platts data showed. October loadings also included one full 500,000-barrel Oman crude cargo that traded in the MOC in August.
The traded volumes for convergence cargoes were down 45.5% month over month and 78.6% year over year. Full crude cargo traded volumes held steady month over month. No trades for crude cargoes were reported for the same year-ago period in the MOC.
Asia crude MOC activity tapered off in August. A total of 187 Dubai crude partials were traded, down 41% month over month and 76% year over year. On the buy side, Mercuria led with 140 partials, followed by Trafigura and Vitol with 13 partials each.
Unipec was the biggest seller with 157 partials, followed by PetroChina and Shell with 21 and 4 partials, respectively.
Performance tracking Platts closely monitors performance on trades reported
through the MOC as part of its ongoing commitment to upholding the highest standards of integrity and transparency.
As part of trade performance tracking, Platts has reviewed various aspects of performance, including the timeliness of nominations, loading completion and other published parameters of the trades.

TAIWAN DATA: September crude imports rise 17.6% YOY as US, Qatari supply increases
US overtakes Saudi as Taiwan's top crude source Qatari shipments resume after three-month gap High freight costs limit February purchase plans

Taiwan's crude oil imports rose 17.6% year over year in September as higher US and Qatari arrivals more than offset steep declines in Saudi Arabian and UAE supply, data from the Ministry of Economic Affairs' Energy Administration released Nov. 2 showed.
Imports totaled 26.427 million barrels, down 1.2% from August's 26.753 million barrels but above 22.464 million barrels a year earlier. On a daily basis, September imports averaged 880,907 b/d, up 2.1% from August's 863,011 b/d, according to the data.
The US became Taiwan's largest crude supplier in September, with arrivals increasing 57.7% month over month and 11.5% year over year to 6.520 million barrels. Its share of total imports rose to 24.7% from 15.5% in August.
Qatar ranked second, supplying 5.876 million barrels, or 22.2% of imports, after no arrivals were recorded from June through August. That compared with 476,072 barrels in September 2025.
Despite the recovery in US arrivals in September, traders said elevated freight rates were constraining purchases for the current February-arrival cycle.
WTI Midland volumes for February 2027 arrival had thinned as high freight costs kept the arbitrage window closed, Singaporebased crude traders had said Sept. 17. Trade data compiled by Platts also showed relatively slower sales of light sweet US crude to Asia in the new trading cycle compared with previous months.

Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, assessed Dirty USGC-China VLCC 270,000 mt rates at $222.22/mt on Nov. 4, down $11.11/mt day ovet day; Dirty USGC-Singapore Suezmax 130,000 mt was assessed at $200/mt, down $15.38/mt over the same period.
Earlier buying activity during September included CPC's procurement of four Aframax cargoes of WTI Midland crude from Gunvor for December delivery, at premiums of about $27/b to Platts Dated Brent crude assessments, CFR Taiwan, the traders said.
CPC was also heard to have bought two Aframax cargoes of Argentina's Medanito crude for December delivery, one each from PetroChina and TotalEnergies, at premiums in the $22s/b to Platts Dated Brent crude assessments, CFR Taiwan, they added.
Formosa Petrochemical bought 2 million barrels of Oman crude through a tender for December loading and delivery, at a premium of about $5/b to Platts November Dubai crude assessments, on an FOB basis plus freight, market sources had said Oct. 16.
In September's import data, Oman was Taiwan's third-largest supplier, with volumes rising 20.8% month over month and 99% year over year to 3.981 million barrels. Kuwaiti arrivals were broadly steady from August at 3.869 million barrels, up 11.9% year over year.
Saudi Arabian supply fell 57.6% month over month and 60.5% year over year to 2.380 million barrels. UAE arrivals declined 53.2% from August to 1.880 million barrels, while Iraqi volumes dropped 67% to 1.922 million barrels.
Refinery intake fell 4.9% month over month to 3.395 million metric tons of oil equivalent in September, although it rose 9.5% year over year, the data showed.
Over January-September, Taiwan's crude imports declined 4.9% year over year to 216.036 million barrels, while refinery intake fell 6.7% to 29.133 million metric tons of oil equivalent.
-- Mia Pei, Alvinn Philips

Woodside moves end-Nov loading North West Shelf condensate cargo
Vietnam's PV Oil offers 250,000 barrels Thang Long crude for Jan 15-20 loading
Taiwan's September crude imports rise 17.6% YOY
The Asia-Pacific sweet crude market saw the emergence of a sell tender from Vietnam's PV Oil, coupled with trading activity on the Australian condensate segment Nov. 5.
On the condensate front, Australian producer Woodside Energy was heard to have moved its North West Shelf condensate cargo for loading Nov. 21-22, although further pricing details remained unascertained at the time of publication. The cargo had initially been scheduled to load Dec. 4-8, but logistical constraints prompted the loading window to be brought forward, several trade sources said.

Platts Middle East Sour Crude Daily Market Analysis
At least 8 million barrels awarded in UAE tender
Basrah Medium likely awarded to Nov. 4 Indian buy tender
December ESPO Blend likely fully placed: sources
Details on a recently awarded Abu Dhabi crude sale tender were circulating in the Middle East crude market Nov. 5, while

traders also noted further movement in Iraqi Basrah crudes. Initial estimates were that up to five VLCCs' worth of Murban
crude were awarded in the Abu Dhabi tender to an oil major and four refiners across South Korea and Japan for loading in both December and January.
Awarded levels for December-loading cargoes were heard at premiums in the $3-$4s/b range to Platts December Dubai crude assessments, FOB, and for January-loading cargoes at premiums in the $4s-$5s/b range to Platts January Dubai crude assessments, FOB.
A 700,000-barrel, December-loading Umm Lulu crude cargo was also heard awarded in the same tender to a trading house, at a premium in the $4s/b range to Platts December Dubai crude assessments, STS Fujairah.
There was talk that two Upper Zakum parcels were also placed outside the tender with refiners in India and China, though further details were unclear.
While some traders felt the levels fetched for Murban were on the high side compared to medium sour crude values, they added that they were nonetheless still economical compared to similar, staple grades from the West.
"I think the landed cost [for WTI Midland] is still very expensive so Murban is currently the best choice," one Singapore-based trader said.
Trade sources also noted that a 1 million-barrel, Novemberloading Basrah Medium cargo likely was awarded into an Indian buy tender that closed Nov. 4, seeking early-December delivery cargoes of Basrah Medium, Khafji or Mars crude. Price levels were unclear.
In the Far East Russian ESPO Blend crude trade, several Chinese refining sources said the December program for the grade has likely been fully placed.
The sources added that the December ESPO Blend program was shorter than the November program, with some estimates at around 10 fewer cargoes, likely due to maintenance, though this could not be confirmed.
Last traded levels were heard at premiums in the $24-$25/b range to ICE Brent crude futures, DES China.
Platts is part of S&P Global Energy. -- Yong ren Toh
Platts Mediterranean & Black Sea Sweet Crude Daily Commentary
Softer freight lifts differentials
CPC searches for Northwest Europe arbitrage
Softening freight rates lifted sentiment in the Mediterranean sweet crude market Nov. 5, pushing differentials higher day over day, but trading activity remained slow, with market participants awaiting the start of the December trading cycle.
"Freight collapsed a lot so that is helping CPC a lot," said one Mediterranean trader. "The extremely high levels of freight were unsustainable, and the prices collapsed in the last few days."

Lower freight costs could open arbitrage opportunities to Northwest Europe for CPC cargoes in December, when the market is expected to be more constructive.
"We are seeing December demand stronger than in November, but it is still a weak market," the trader said.
But other sources pointed out that softer freight also creates arbitrage opportunities into Europe, putting local grades under pressure.
"Softer freight means there are a lot of arbitrage barrels coming into Europe, particularly WTI Midland, which are putting CPC and Azeri Light under pressure," said a second said.
Sellers are still trying to place end-of-November and early December volumes, but they struggle to get the prices they want.
"CPC is offered for 1D December at minus $7/barrel [to Dated Brent] and Azeri Light is offered for 3D November at plus $10/b, but there is no trading," said a third Mediterranean source. "That's what is on offer today, but I think it is too high."
With sellers still working to place their volumes, CPC and Azeri cargoes are still available for end-of-November loading, market participants said.
Although softer freight has provided some support to sweet differentials, it remains to be seen whether cheaper freight will reopen arbitrage opportunities for CPC Blend, or whether it will come under renewed pressure from the steady flow of US barrels.
Even if sentiment has edged higher, the market remains depressed, with CPC Suezmax CIF Augusta differentials at alltime lows. Opening of the arbitrage to Northwest Europe would provide some relief in the near term, but mercaptan limits could prevent refineries from buying too many CPC cargoes, market sources said previously.

Platts North Sea Crude Daily Market Analysis
Selloff stalls as buyers step in
Freight softens; sparks bidding interest
Buying interest emerged in the North Sea crude oil market Nov. 5 in response to an easing freight environment, softer differentials and a conducive market structure.
This followed weeks of sell-side pressure that resulted in a sharp retreat for physical differentials.
The Platts Market on Close assessment process saw three trades in the North Sea crude oil market.
Vitol lifted a Trafigura offer for a Forties cargo arriving in Rotterdam over Nov. 19-23 at a premium of 75 cents/b over Dated Brent. Vitol also lifted a Petroineos offer for a WTI Midland cargo arriving over Nov. 20-24 at a premium of $3.20/b over Dated Brent.
Another Trafigura offer for a Brent Blend cargo loading over Dec. 2-4 was lifted by bp at a discount of $1.15/b to Dated Brent.
The wider over-the-counter market was also heard to be active during the session across both Forties and WTI Midland markets, two North Sea crude traders said.
"[It] seems that cargoes have begun to flow with the correction," said one trader, pointing to the recent selloff in the

North Sea crude market. Freight rates, while still relatively high, have seen a sharp
decline this week. Platts assessed the dirty cross-UK-Continent Aframax at $37.74/mt Nov. 5, down $1.33/mt on the day and $22.85/mt on the week.
The Platts-assessed US Gulf Coast-UK/continent Aframax freight rate fell $10.51/mt on the day to $113.45/mt Nov. 4.
"All freight is coming off so much, making the physical market active again," the trader said.
The contango in the prompt end of the Brent CFD derivative curve has also enticed buyers, providing a less punitive environment for traders to lift prompt cargoes for deferred demand, traders have noted.
A second North Sea crude trader described spot cargo differentials as "quite cheap," and also highlighted the easing freight costs as a driver for demand.
Platts is part of S&P Global Energy. -- Ernest Puey
Platts West African Crude Daily Commentary
WAF sentiment still firmer as shipping costs fall
Ongoing buy tenders stifle spot trading activity
West African crude oil sentiment remained more supportive Nov. 5 as freight rates continued their softer trajectory, though spot trade was heard still limited as market participants awaited results from several buy tenders.
Platts assessed the Dirty West Africa-UK Continent 130,000-metric-ton fixture, the typical route taken by WAF crude into Northwest Europe, at $74/mt on Nov. 5, down by $18.93/mt day on day, and down from $172.10/mt a month previously.
"Freight rates are thankfully coming off, and [the Brent market] structure too, so it gives anyone in WAF some hope," one Europe-based WAF trader said on the day. "It's hard to put a finger on trade levels with very little information on recent deals."
Trading sources highlighted several ongoing buy tenders for which the results could give insight into the health of the wider market. One of the tenders was from Nigeria's own Dangote refinery, which was seeking December deliveries of crude oil, sources said.
"This week there have not been many offers, perhaps because they are in tenders," a second WAF trader said. "Freight is rather low and margins are not bad. There are not too many things that could put pressure on premiums, unless freight rebounds aggressively and suddenly."
While freight costs softened, of concern for Nigerian barrels remained the high volume of competitively priced alternatives to European refiners, as pricing in the North Sea and Mediterranean markets was heard moving lower.
For those grades in West Africa that typically find buyers in Asia, competing Brazilian barrels were said by sources to be landing in China at prices that lend little support to free-on-board cash differentials for Angolan and Congolese grades.
Platts is part of S&P Global Energy. -- George Delaney

Americas Crude
Landed WTI Midland prices in Europe stabilize at multi-month lows
Freight costs plunge on US Gulf route
Buying interest emerges, supports floor
December cargo premiums drop to $6/barrel
Prices for WTI Midland deliveries into Europe have fallen to multi-month lows on Nov. 5, although emerging buying interest could limit further declines.
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, assessed the WTI Midland CIF Rotterdam differential at a premium of $3.08/barrel over Dated Brent at the Nov. 5 close, the lowest since Sept. 4, when it was assessed at a $1.685/b premium.
Later arrivals into Europe have also seen similar weakness. Platts assessed the WTI Midland DAP Rotterdam differential at an average of $6/b premium to the Dated Brent benchmark on Nov. 5, its lowest level since Sept. 7, when it was assessed at a $5.20/b premium.
Transatlantic freight costs have plunged in recent sessions, with further downside anticipated among shipping market participants in response to tonnage availability on the US Gulf Coast.
Platts assessed the US Gulf Coast-UK/continent Aframax freight rate at $113.45/metric ton Nov. 4, down $10.51/mt day over day and $36.77/mt week over week.
Trading activity during the Platts Market on Close assessment process in recent sessions has reflected a slowdown in the selloff of landed differentials.
Traders also indicated weakening values for mid-December arriving cargoes through the first week of November.
However, the emergence of buying interest has kept a floor under prices. Levels for mid-December arriving cargoes were heard declining from around an $8/b premium to December Dated Brent at the start of the week to around a $6/b premium to December Dated Brent mid-week.
"[Differentials] should be at least stable now that we see the buyers approaching with [freight] coming off," a North Sea crude oil trader said.
-- Oliwier Wronski, Ernest Puey
FACTBOX: US crude, LPG gain footprint in Asia amid shipping and supply risks
Elevated freight has changed comparative economics
South Korea, Japan sharply boost US crude purchases
US LPG flows to India surge more than fourfold
US crude and LPG flows to Asia are rising sharply as refiners and consumers seek to reduce their exposure to disruptions affecting Middle Eastern production and shipping routes, particularly around the Strait of Hormuz.

While the shift is more pronounced in North Asia, where South Korea and Japan have sharply increased US crude purchases, South Asian buyers are also expanding access to US supplies, with LPG flows witnessing an exponential rise.
However, US barrels are not replacing Middle Eastern crude outright. Instead, refiners are combining traditional Gulf supplies with US, Canadian and Latin American grades to improve supply resilience, manage crude-quality requirements and secure timely deliveries. The willingness to pay a premium for Atlantic Basin cargoes has increased as freight, insurance and replacement costs rise for shipments exposed to disruption.
"Longer voyages, higher freight and different crude qualities all add to cost and complexity, so US supply works best as a complement to Gulf barrels rather than a substitute," said Premasish Das, executive director for oil analytics at S&P Global Energy CERA.
India's oil procurement strategy also illustrates a broader shift. The country has increased both US crude and LPG purchases while building inventories and processing more crude to meet domestic festival demand and international demand for middle distillates.
Pakistan, on the other hand, is examining additional US and West African supplies as shipping delays complicate procurement from traditional suppliers.
Trade flows North Asian refiners are taking record or near-record
volumes of US crude, while India has sharply expanded US LPG purchases and continues to use US crude as a strategic diversification source. Japan's US crude imports rose to 532,776 b/d in September,
almost 15 times the 36,183 b/d recorded a year earlier. US crude accounted for 27.5% of Japan's total crude imports of 1.94 million b/d that month. January-September US imports averaged a record 400,642 b/d, up 403% year over year, with cumulative purchases reaching 109.4 million barrels. South Korea is poised to maintain its status as Asia's top US crude buyer in 2026 as it imported 144.23 million barrels during January-September, up 19.1% from a year earlier. US crude was South Korea's second-largest source after Saudi Arabia in the first nine months, while total imports from the Americas reached a record 191.4 million barrels over the period, representing 26.12% of crude arrivals.

© 2026 by S&P Global Inc. All rights reserved.

8

©2026 S&P Global Inc. Any unauthorized use of this copyrighted material constitutes infringement of our intellectual property and will subject you to possible legal action.

Platts Oilgram Price Report

November 5, 2026

Indian refiners imported a record 5.54 million b/d of crude in October, 9% higher than in both September and October 2025. US crude flows averaged 125,000 b/d during JanuaryOctober 2026 and reached 150,000 b/d in October, up 16% from September. The US supplied an average of 238,000 b/d in 2025, compared with 149,000 b/d in 2024, although 2026 flows have been more uneven.
India's LPG sourcing has changed more sharply. Middle Eastern suppliers accounted for about 56% of India's LPG imports during January-October, down from approximately 93% during the same period in 2025. US LPG supplies increased more than fourfold over the same period, from 1.2 million mt to more than 5 million mt, lifting the US share from about 7% to 35%.
Cnergyico in Pakistan plans to continue sourcing crude from West Africa and the US alongside Middle Eastern suppliers. The refiner received 1 million barrels of WTI Midland in late October. Alternative supplies are being assessed based on crude compatibility, freight, voyage time, and total landed cost.
Other Asian buyers are also turning to US crude. Taiwan's CPC purchased six Aframax cargoes of WTI Midland for January arrival, while Japan's ENEOS bought December-arrival WTI Midland at premiums of around $20/b to Dubai. Thailand, Taiwan, Japan and South Korea have all identified US crude as a leading non-Middle Eastern supply option.
Prices
Although US grades command high headline premiums, elevated Persian Gulf-to-Asia freight and insurance costs have changed their comparative economics.
WTI Midland premiums remain elevated. Platts assessed the light sweet US grade on a DES Yeosu basis at a premium of $31.50/b to front-month Dubai on Nov. 4, after reaching a record-high premium of $34.60/b on Sept. 18.
Delivery certainty is commanding a premium. Asian refiners say the additional cost of US crude reflects its reliable availability and lower exposure to tanker disruptions around the Strait of Hormuz. Freight, insurance, and the cost of replacing delayed cargoes are increasingly being factored into procurement decisions.
However, some Asian refiners find US crude competitive on a delivered basis. Despite high Atlantic Basin premiums, WTI Midland has averaged a $6.45/b discount to Abu Dhabi's Murban on a CFR North Asia basis so far this year. This is compared with an average discount of just 18 cents/b in 2025, reflecting higher Persian Gulf-to-Asia freight and insurance costs.
Light, sweet US grades offer strong middle-distillate yields, making them particularly attractive to Japan ahead of the winter kerosene-demand season. Canadian Cold Lake and Access Western Blend have also attracted interest in South Korea because their heavy, sour qualities can substitute for constrained Basrah Medium, Arab Medium and Arab Heavy supplies.

Some commercial limits remain. US crude cannot fully replace Middle Eastern grades because Asian complex refineries require a broad range of crude qualities. For India and Pakistan, the final decision continues to depend on the relative cost of US barrels, freight, refinery compatibility and the duration of regional disruptions.
Infrastructure High freight costs and premiums remain constraints, but
delivery certainty, route security and supply resilience are carrying greater weight in procurement decisions.
The Panama route could improve the economics of US crude. South Korea's customs authorities are working to extend preferential treatment under the South Korea-US free trade agreement to US crude moved through Panama's Petroterminal de Panamá pipeline. The arrangement would support the transfer of crude from Panama's Atlantic coast to its Pacific coast before loading it onto tankers bound for Asia.
The Panama route could reduce the journey from the US Gulf Coast to East Asia from approximately 55 days via the Cape of Good Hope to about 40 days. Estimated VLCC transportation costs could decline from $28.3 million to $24.3 million.
Petroterminal de Panamá's trans-isthmian pipeline is about 130 km long and moves crude between Atlantic and Pacific terminals. Its reported capacity is approximately 10 million barrels per month, providing an alternative to the longer route around southern Africa.
Refiners are adapting their logistics as well as their sourcing strategies. South Korean authorities have simplified documentation requirements for certain US crude shipments transiting neighboring countries, while discussions with Panama could enable wider use of the Pacific route. -- Sambit Mohanty, Philip Vahn
Canadian Natural increases capacity on TMX oil pipeline to 177,750 b/d
CNR boosts TMX pipeline commitment to 265,250 b/d
Horizon turnaround adds 23,000 b/d SCO output
Thermal facilities hit record 300,000 b/d mark
Canadian Natural Resources has increased its committed volumes on the Trans Mountain Expansion pipeline to 177,750 barrels/day, on the back of incremental output and aimed at achieving higher netbacks for its heavy oil volumes, President Scott Stauth said Nov. 5.
"The increased capacity on TMX came through the process where TMC came out and moved volumes from spot to firm as part of the overall toll settlement process," Stauth said on the company's third-quarter 2026 earnings webcast.
Trans Mountain Corp. is the operator of the 890,000 b/d TMX that ships heavy and light crude oil from Edmonton, Alberta, to the West Ridge Marine Terminal at Burnaby in coastal British Columbia.

© 2026 by S&P Global Inc. All rights reserved.

9

©2026 S&P Global Inc. Any unauthorized use of this copyrighted material constitutes infringement of our intellectual property and will subject you to possible legal action.

Platts Oilgram Price Report

November 5, 2026

CNR has been a committed shipper on this line and has a capacity of 169,000 b/d (increased to 177,750 b/d), according to information on the company's website.
Besides TMX, CNR also has capacity on the South Bow Corp.'s Keystone pipeline that transports Western Canadian crude to the US Midwest and US Gulf Coast.
The company's combined contracted oil export transportation capacity to Canada's West Coast and the USGC now stands at 265,250 b/d, representing nearly 22% of 2026 forecasted liquids production, CNR said.
"The egress supports Canadian Natural's long-term sales strategy by targeting diverse refining markets which drive stronger netbacks while also reducing exposure to egress constraints. We [also] have contracts for ships to move our product," Stauth said.
His statements came with CNR announcing a third-quarter 2026 output of 1.652 million b/d of oil equivalent, of which crude oil and NGL were 1.217 million boe/d and natural gas at 2.6 billion cubic feet/day, the company said.
"Q3 2026 was another strong quarter for Canadian Natural, highlighting the strength of our long-life, low-decline asset base and our continued focus on safe, reliable and effective operations. Our diverse portfolio delivered resilient production through the quarter even with the planned turnaround at the Horizon [oil sands facility]," he said.
Horizon to add new SCO output Completion of the Horizon turnaround in 28 days in Q3, 2026
will add about 23,000 b/d of incremental high-margin synthetic crude oil output in the current quarter and complement the company's overall oil sands mining and upgrading operations that now stand at 640,000 b/d, Stauth said.
Synthetic crude oil is an upgraded heavy oil that fetches a premium to WTI for refiners.
The SCO price premium to WTI last quarter averaged $6.65/b, primarily driven by strong diesel refining margins and planned seasonal maintenance in the Western Canadian Sedimentary Basin, the earnings release said.
"Based on the success of the 2024 reliability enhancement project and analysis completed during the 2026 turnaround, we are now extending the turnaround intervals at Horizon from once every two years to once every three years. This continuous improvement initiative increases average production capacity by 5,000 b/d of SCO over the three-year cycle and further enhances the reliability and top-tier competitiveness of our oil sands mining and upgrading operations," he said.
Also in the last quarter, CNR achieved several production records, including 300,000 b/d output from its thermal in-situ (steam-assisted gravity drainage) facilities, a 25,000 b/d increase over the same quarter of 2025, Stauth said.
At Jackfish, the company achieved a production of 143,000 b/d, exceeding nameplate capacity by 23,000 b/d, he said, noting that output averaged last quarter at 46,500 b/d at the Pike 1 facility.

Dealing with WCS, WTI differentials Operating netbacks of Canadian oil sands producers, including
CNR, have been impacted by the recent widening of the Western Canadian Select and WTI differentials, Stauth said.
"The impacts of the increased Venezuelan [oil] production and the high shipping rates would be the factors that we see as having some implications to the widening of the differential. Fundamentally, if you look at the spread between [WCS, WTI] Hardisty and [WCS, WTI] Houston, that value is still an approximate the transportation cost. So, I don't think we're seeing the system as being completely full," he said.
A planned capacity addition of 90,000 b/d on the TMX pipeline, achieved through the use of drag-reducing agents by early 2027, will start "taking effect" by narrowing the differential, Stath said.
"The positive sign is that the DRA is going to add additional barrels going to the West Coast and I feel pretty good about where we're at in terms of pricing. Also, we'll see what happens as we go forward with WTI," he said.
-- Ashok Dutta
South Bow says FID for 450,000 b/d Prairie Connector line by mid-2027
Unveils C$65 million spend on pre-FID studies
Q3 Keystone throughput stood at 586,000 b/d
South Bow Corp. is working towards a final investment decision by mid-2027 for the 450,000-barrel/day Prairie Connector crude oil pipeline, with the company unveiling a capital spend of C$65 million ($46 million) in pre-FID work, CEO Bevin Wirzba said Nov. 5.
"Outlook for the Western Canadian Sedimentary Basin continues to strengthen," Wirzba said on the third-quarter 2026 earnings webcast. "Production is growing, and importantly, our conversation with customers is becoming increasingly focused on how we can support their growth and expansion plans. We see opportunities from additional connectivity and expansions of our existing infrastructure to larger-scale projects."
Prairie Connector is a planned project to transport Canadian crude oil from Hardisty, Alberta, via a direct route to Cushing, Oklahoma, and the US Gulf Coast by utilizing existing pipeline capacity and right-of-way, according to information on the South Bow website.
In March, South Bow launched an open season seeking firm commitments.
"We heard through the open season that our customers look to grow production and scale and they need additional egress to establish markets," he said, noting that South Bow remains focused on its growth strategy, including its proposed Prairie Connector project and the joint development of the proposed Liberty Bridge Pipeline project with Bridger.
Key activities that remain underway include advancing permitting and land acquisitions, pursuing government assurances regarding permit durability, progressing execution

High utilization rates Separately, throughput volumes averaged 586,000 b/d last

Throughput on the Marketlink pipeline averaged 642,000 b/d in the third quarter of 2026, it said.
The 2,700-mile Keystone Pipeline System ships crude from Western Canada to refineries in the US Midwest and the USGC. The Canadian section runs from Hardisty to North Dakota and through to Steele City, Nebraska, where it splits into two sections, with the first headed through Missouri for deliveries to refineries into Wood River and Patoka in Illinois and the other south through Oklahoma to Cushing and to Port Arthur and Houston. The second section of the pipeline system, called Marketlink, delivers crude from Cushing to refineries in Sour Lake, Houston, Port Arthur, and Port Neches, all in Texas.

Norway's Equinor to advance oil exploration in southern Argentina's offshore
Aim is to drill in 2028-2029 Partnering with YPF on the projects More companies seeking offshore resources
Equinor said Nov. 5 it will advance with partners to the second stage of exploration in two offshore blocks in southern Argentina, where the Norway-based company plans to drill its first wells between 2028 and 2029.
The exploration will be in the AUS 105 and AUS 106 blocks in the southernmost Austral basin, the company said.

It will explore AUS 105 with Argentina's state-backed YPF and Compañía General de Combustibles, and AUS 106 with YPF.
"Argentina is an important part of Equinor's international exploration portfolio and offers attractive long-term opportunities for the development of offshore resources, in line with our international strategy," the Oslo-based company said.
These are the first companies to advance to the second stage of exploration on blocks secured in a 2017 licensing round, a sign of the potential for finds as other companies test to the north in offshore Uruguay on the maritime border with Argentina.
Equinor shifted its focus in Argentina to offshore earlier this year, selling its assets in the onshore Vaca Muerta shale block to Mexico-based Vista Energy, the third-largest oil producer in Argentina.
The sale earned it $1.1 billion, which the company said at the time would be used in other core international markets including Brazil, the UK and the US. It added at the time that it would continue to explore offshore Argentina.
Equinor drilled its first offshore well in the North Argentina Basin in 2024. While the well came up dry, Equinor said it gained data for future drilling there, and YPF said earlier this year that it will drill future wells.
More companies have been looking at offshore areas in the North Argentina Basin and neighboring Uruguay since large offshore oil discoveries were made in Namibia starting in 2022. Equinor has stakes in the CAN 100 and CAN 114 blocks in the North Argentina Basin, as well as acreage in the Malvinas Oeste basin in the south near the Austral basin.
-- Charles Newbery
Murphy Oil boosts production guidance on Eagle Ford strength
Q3 output hits 186,495 boe/d, tops forecast
Eagle Ford investment jumps to $500M in 2027
Chinook, Vietnam projects yield $1B cash flow
Murphy Oil raised its full-year 2026 production guidance after third-quarter output topped its forecast range, and CEO Eric Hambly said the company expects to lift Eagle Ford Shale spending to around $500 million in 2027.
Houston-based Murphy reported Q3 production of 186,495 boe/d, excluding noncontrolling interest, above its guidance of 171,000-179,000 boe/d, in its Nov. 4 earnings release. Oil output averaged 84,164 b/d. Total production rose from 168,995 boe/d in Q2 but was down from 200,383 boe/d a year-earlier.
The company attributed the beat to accelerated Eagle Ford activity, new well outperformance and lower royalties in Canada's Tupper Montney, and minimal storm downtime in the US Gulf.
Murphy raised its full-year production guidance to 176,000178,000 boe/d from 167,000-175,000 boe/d, about 48% oil, and kept capital spending guidance at $1.5 billion-$1.6 billion. It guided Q4 output to 174,000-182,000 boe/d, which it said reflects a reduction of about 3,700 boe/d from Hurricane Isaias.

"Oil prices remained elevated in a constructive but volatile market defined by geopolitical disruption," Hambly said in a letter to stockholders. "Our unhedged position allowed Murphy to capture the full benefit of those prices."
Murphy's realized oil price averaged $89.35/b in Q3, according to the letter. Its US onshore oil realization was $86.48/b, down from $99.55/b in Q2 and up from $65.48/b a year-earlier. The company listed "the ongoing conflict in the Middle East involving Iran" among the risks in its forward-looking statements.
On a Nov. 5 earnings call, Hambly said the company's newest projects at Chinook and Lac Da Vang are expected to generate about $1 billion in combined free cash flow over the next five years at $80/b Brent.
Chinook 8 came online Sept. 22 through Murphy's Pioneer FPSO in the US Gulf, and Lac Da Vang offshore Vietnam achieved first oil Sept. 27, the company said. Chris Lorino, senior vice president of operations and HSE, said Chinook 8 is expected to provide about 9,000 b/d of oil in Q4. He said the gain will be partly offset by hurricane downtime, offshore workovers and maintenance at Delta House.
Hambly said Lac Da Vang is expected to produce 3,000-5,000 b/d net to Murphy in 2027, exit 2027 at 7,000-12,000 b/d and peak at 10,000-15,000 b/d in 2028 or 2029.
Murphy has not set its 2027 budget and will present it on its Q4 call in late January, Hambly said.
"Directionally we're looking at Eagle Ford capital increasing from sort of the plus or minus $300 million mark in the last year or two to around $500 million, somewhere in that ballpark, in 2027," he said.
Hambly said the company plans a steady Eagle Ford program next year with production peaking in Q4. Over the past five years, the program was largely complete by Q3. Murphy cut Eagle Ford well costs per lateral foot by 26% as of 2025. Hambly said its 2026 completion cost per foot was the lowest in the company's Eagle Ford history, "lower than 2016 when service costs were very low, lower than 2021 when service costs were low."
He said exploration is expected to account for 10%-15% of Murphy's 2027 capital program. Appraisal of the Bubale discovery offshore Cote d'Ivoire will drive "the bulk of our range of uncertainty" in next year's budget, he said. Appraisal wells are

likely to cost $80 million-$100 million each, and Murphy expects to begin the program in Q2 2027, Hambly said.
Hambly said Murphy's screening work suggests a standalone Bubale development would require more than 150 million barrels, but he declined to give a new resource estimate before further appraisal.
Hambly said the company expects operating expenses of $10-$12/boe in 2027. Murphy said it expects full-year 2026 lease operating expenses toward the lower end of that range.
On shareholder returns, Hambly said Murphy is likely to buy back stock if its share price "pulls back significantly lower than even a fairly conservative view of intrinsic value," while protecting the balance sheet. He said the company will be "monitoring oil price, as our share price moves a lot with oil price."
Murphy did not repurchase shares in Q3 and has $550 million remaining under its authorization. Total debt was $1.63 billion at the end of the quarter.
Q3 net income attributable to Murphy was $171.1 million, or $1.17/share, compared with a loss of $3.0 million, or 2 cents/ share, a year earlier. Adjusted net income was $158.4 million, or $1.08/share.

Gasoline
Dangote raises gasoline prices, widening arbitrage into Nigeria
Refinery shifts to fixed naira pricing
Import arbitrage widens to 8-month high
The Dangote refinery has sharply raised its coastal and gantry gasoline sales price and changed its pricing mechanism, according to sources and reports published by the refinery and seen by Platts, part of S&P Global Energy.
The refinery raised the FOB coastal gasoline sales price from an $80/metric ton premium to Eurobob pricing to a fixed price of 1,676,250 nairas/mt (1,246/mt) at 12 am Nov. 3. It also raised the truck gantry price from a $130/mt premium to Eurobob pricing to 1,300 nairas/liter (97 cents/l).
Traders said the price increase was due to a wide spread between the previous gantry sales price and retail prices, which had not adjusted down despite lower international prices.
"[Dangote] sees that marketers were not dropping their pump price as flat prices were dropping, and it was seeing how much margin they were making," a trader said.
The Platts-assessed STS Lome gasoline price has fallen steadily over the past several weeks, from $1,281.75/mt on Oct. 12 to $1,020.75/mt on Nov. 4.
The decline has put the spread between FOB West Africa and STS Lome at a $236.75/mt premium as of Nov. 4, the highest since March 10.
Freight rates within West Africa remain very high, making the arbitrage inland more challenging. Platts assessed the cross-WAF

Gasoline imports to US Atlantic Coast hold steady with New England focus
New England ports receive 6.6M barrels total
Canada, Norway each supply half of USAC imports
RBOB differential drops 0.60 cent at NYH
Gasoline imports to the US Atlantic Coast in early November held steady compared with recent months, with strong flows continuing into New England, recent US Customs data showed Nov. 5.
The recently released data showed that all November gasoline cargoes bound for PADD 1 (USAC) were offloaded at ports in Maine and Massachusetts. Gasoline imports to date in the region have totaled 622,646 barrels, the data showed, with about half of those volumes originating from Canada and the other half from Norway.
Gasoline cargoes continue to favor New England ports, with 6.6 million barrels of gasoline imported to Maine, Massachusetts, Connecticut and Rhode Island, compared to 2.3 million barrels arriving at the New York Harbor area in New York and New Jersey, according to the data.
Recent data from the US Energy Information Administration showed a week-over-week drop in gasoline imports to the USAC, with gasoline inflows for the week ended Oct. 29 at 284,000 barrels/day, down from 347,000 b/d the prior week.
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, assessed the differential for RBOB gasoline loading onto barges at New York Harbor 0.60 cent lower on Nov. 5, at NYMEX December RBOB futures plus 8-90 cents/gal, while the outright price rose 2.88 cents to $2.8524/gallon.
The rising price came a day after USAC regular gasoline tumbled below $3/gal for the first time since March, Platts data showed.
-- Jordan Daniel
USAC spot gasoline drops below $3/gal for first time since March
Gasoline prices fall to March lows
Premium grade differentials collapse rapidly
East Coast refinery utilization jumps 7.8%
USAC regular gasoline tumbled below $3/gal on Nov. 4 for the first time since March, largely on a falling RBOB futures market but also on new data showing increased refinery run rates.

Platts assessed RBOB and CBOB barges down 50 points to NYMEX December RBOB futures plus 9.50 cents/gal, or an outright price of $2.8236/gal, dropping 50.79 cents just this week to the lowest level since March 25. Buckeye Pipeline barrels dropped 1.75 cents to plus 7.75 cents/gal. Front-line RBOB futures tumbled 20.69 cents to $2.7286/gal, their lowest since March 10 but still much higher than one year ago when they were at $1.9228/gal.
"Crazy day. They just started clobbering the market more," one USAC gasoline trader said. "It's moving too much, too fast. It's a game of chicken and people want to sell at these higher levels."
Differentials slipped alongside the underlying NYMEX futures, starting the week at 17.5 cents over the NYMEX for RBOB barges.
Premium gasoline grades were even more affected, staying 24.75 cents over regular gasoline Oct. 4, but falling 32.25 cents the day before, the biggest one-day drop since 36 cents on April 2, 2024.
"Premium grades are nuts right now," another AC trader said. "Everything is all over the place."
"Octanes have collapsed," a third trader said. "They were pretty high historically anyway, but they're coming down pretty fast."
Data released Nov. 4 by the US Energy Information Administration showed East Coast refinery utilization for the week ending Oct. 30 rose 7.8 percentage points to 89.5 percent, the highest level since May 15. Gasoline production was nearly flat on the week and year over year at 3.2 million b/d, while stocks dipped 1.85 million barrels to 52.1 million barrels, on par with yearago levels.
East Coast gasoline imports continued to drop, down 63,000 b/d to 284,000 b/d, although that is up 5 percent from the previous year. US Customs data showed Irving Oil unloaded 25,000 barrels of premium RBOB Nov. 1 into Boston from Canada, while Reliance unloaded 519,592 barrels of alkylate blendstock into Newark Oct. 30 from India.

Japan cuts fuel subsidies by 1% for Nov. 5-11 on weaker crude
Subsidy down 50 Japanese sen/liter at ¥51.90/liter
Gasoline pump prices fall to ¥170/liter
Target price increases ¥5/liter from December
Japan cut its fuel subsidy for oil products to ¥51.90/liter (33 cents/liter) for the week of Nov. 5-11, from ¥52.40/liter the previous week, amid weaker crude oil prices, the government said Nov. 5.
The government calculates the weekly subsidy using data that includes the average retail gasoline price, as surveyed every Monday, crude oil prices and other factors. It currently provides a full subsidy for the portion exceeding ¥170/liter ($1.08/liter).
The national average retail price for regular gasoline fell 10 Japanese sen/liter week over week to ¥170/liter as of Nov. 2, marking the second straight week of decline, according to the Oil Information Center of the Institute of Energy Economics, Japan.

The Ministry of Economy, Trade and Industry used Nikkei Dubai as the benchmark for calculating subsidies from Jan. 27, 2022, to March 18, 2026, and ICE Brent from March 19 to June 3.
METI informed trading houses and wholesalers in a May 27 email, seen by Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, that the subsidy amount would be calculated using Platts Dubai as the benchmark, starting with payments made June 4.
The Platts front-month cash Dubai assessment fell 0.4% week over week to $113.50/barrel over Oct. 27-Nov. 2.
The government announced Oct. 29 that it will begin raising its gasoline target price of about ¥170/liter by ¥5/liter every month from December through early spring to gradually reduce subsidies.
Nobuhiko Kikkawa, director of fuel distribution and retail office in the Agency for Natural Resources and Energy, part of METI, said at a press briefing Oct. 30 that the target price will rise by ¥2/liter in the week of Dec. 3-9 and Dec. 10-16, ¥1/liter in the week of Dec. 17-23 and remain unchanged in the week of Dec. 24-30, while there is no predetermined plan for the target price in January 2027 and after as the government will keep a close eye on crude oil prices and the situation in the Middle East.
The government ended its gasoline subsidies on Dec. 30, 2025, but reinstated them on March 11 following a surge in crude prices driven by the Middle East conflict. Meanwhile, subsidies for kerosene, gasoil, and fuel oil have remained in place since the program was launched in January 2022.
Refiners use the subsidy to curb increases in the weekly wholesale prices of oil products, while trading houses deduct it from the selling prices of imported oil products.
The Platts-assessed average prices across the Chiba, Kanagawa, Chukyo, and Hanshin regions on Nov. 5 are as follows: gasoline at ¥97,100/kiloliter ($97.76/b), kerosene at ¥114,800/kl, gasoil at ¥100,700/kl, low-sulfur A-fuel oil at ¥99,300/kl and A-fuel oil at ¥98,800/kl.

Platts Asia and Middle Eastern Gasoline Daily Market Analysis
US gasoline stocks fall to 208.958 million barrels
September Taiwan gasoline consumption rises 2.7% MOM

The Asian gasoline complex is expected to be rangebound to softer on Nov. 5, as market participants continue to expect higher Chinese exports in November, despite the US RBOB-Brent crack widening day over day, following a fall in US gasoline stocks.
The US RBOB-Brent crack was pegged at $19.95/barrel at 0300 GMT Nov 5, edging upward from the Platts assessment of $19.32/b at the Asian close Nov. 4.

The US RBOB-Brent crack widened amid a drop in total US gasoline stocks to 208.958 million barrels in the week to Oct. 30, from 209.180 million barrels the week before, the US Energy Information Administration data showed.
Total US gasoline supplied, a proxy for demand, also rose to 8.808 million barrels/day in the same week from 8.358 million b/d the week before, according to EIA data.
Sweeping Democratic Party victories in the US midterm elections eased concerns about further escalation of the US-Iran conflict, Platts reported earlier.
Brokers pegged the front-month FOB Singapore 92 RON gasoline swaps crack spread with Brent swaps in the $8.20$8.25/b range at 0300 GMT, down day over day from the Platts assessment of $9.52/b at the Nov. 4 Asian close.
Broking sources also placed the physical FOB Singapore 92 RON gasoline crack against front-month ICE Brent crude oil futures in the $5.85-$5.90/b range at 0300 GMT, down from the Platts assessment of $6.92/b at the Nov. 4 close.
Taiwan's motor gasoline consumption rose 2.7% month over month and 3.9% year over year to 580,345 mt in September, despite a midmonth rise in retail prices.
CPC and Formosa Petrochemical raised pump prices by 70 Taiwan cents/liter to T$31.20/liter for 92 RON gasoline for the week started Sept. 14, according to local media.
Platts is part of S&P Global Energy. -- Joshua Ong

Jet
European jet fuel imports from India rise to a one-year high amid a tight market
India ships 310,500 mt of jet fuel to Europe
European prices surge to $1,606/mt, doubling year over year
ARA stocks drop to their lowest level since 2014
Europe's jet fuel imports from India spiked to a one-year high in October amid tight supply due to disrupted flows from the Middle East and strong East-West arbitrage incentives, with European prices remaining high.
Platts' average Jet CIF Northwest Europe cargo price in October stood at $1,606/metric ton, more than double the October 2025 average of about $723/mt. Platts is part of S&P Global Energy.
About 310,500 metric tons of jet fuel arrived in Europe from India in October, the highest monthly volumes seen since December 2025, with a further 105,000 mt expected to arrive in November so far, according to Nov. 5 data from S&P Global Commodities at Sea.
Of the October volumes, 211,600 mt arrived in the Mediterranean, while the remaining 98,900 mt was delivered into Northwest Europe, the data showed.
Flows from India to Europe had plunged since the EU banned imports of refined oil products made from Russian crude in third countries as part of the EU's 18th sanctions package, which took effect Jan. 21.
In February, 94,600 mt of Indian jet fuel arrived in Europe, according to CAS, after which flows dried up. Volumes then gradually began to pick up after the US-Iran war disrupted Middle East volumes.
European jet market players said that while some market participants remain hesitant to accept barrels from India due to the EU sanctions, the rise in flows can be attributed to an acutely tight supply picture and stronger East-West arbitrage incentives.

"Although the arbitrage window for jet fuel from India is open, traders still face difficulties placing Indian-origin volumes in Europe. Some buyers are willing to accept the cargoes, while others are not, making the trade a continued risk," a trader said.
A second market player said some Indian jet fuel volumes arrived in Italy under contract, while others were sold on the spot market to Spain, together accounting for the higher overall volumes. "The main question remains whether buyers will accept Indian-origin volumes," he said.
"It was never about the sanctions; it's about the arbs, and now there is just more of an incentive to send more barrels west," said a third source.
Despite the seasonal softening of European jet demand in October, which some market participants said was prolonged by warmer-than-usual weather, the supply-demand balance remained tight.
Amsterdam-Rotterdam-Antwerp jet fuel and kerosene fuel stocks fell to 3.03 million barrels in October, the lowest level since September 2014, according to Insights Global data.
-- Aruni Sunil, Julia Shcheulova
US DATA: Refinery jet yields grow despite dwindling ULSD stocks
ULSD stocks lowest since 2013
Jet exports rise to 311,000 b/d
Utilization rates remain at 94.50%
US refinery jet fuel yields rose in the week ended Oct. 30, according to estimates derived from Energy Information Administration data released Nov. 4. The increase came as distillate regrades favored jet fuel at the Gulf Coast, where export loadings surged.
Jet fuel accounted for 11.80% of US refinery production, up 0.80 percentage point from the previous week. Estimated yields of ultra-low sulfur diesel fell 0.40 point to 30.80%, down 0.60 point from two weeks earlier.
The US jet-to-ULSD regrade at the US Gulf Coast was estimated at 7.29 cents/gal in favor of jet fuel last week, down from 14.59 cents/gal the previous week. The regrade may be encouraging higher jet fuel output despite historically low ULSD inventories, according to Platts data.
US jet output rose to a two-month high of 1.969 million b/d last week, the EIA said, which is up by 149,000 b/d from the week prior and higher by 252,000 b/d year over year.
ULSD stock levels fell to 88.184 million barrels last week, the lowest since the week to Nov. 22, 2013.
Jet stocks were measured at 41.782 million barrels, down 85,000 barrels year over year and the lowest since March 6, when they stood at 41.245 million barrels.
In contrast to US regrade calculations, the European jetdiesel regrade fell into negative territory over the week to Oct. 30, favoring diesel production. The regrade was assessed at minus $2.38/b on Oct. 30, down from plus $1.56/b on Oct. 23, a $3.94/b

Platts Asia and Middle Eastern Jet Daily Market Analysis
Indonesia's Pertamina seeks 600,000 barrels of jet A-1 fuel for Dec
Switzerland allows for second release of jet fuel stocks
The Asian jet fuel/kerosene market structure strengthened Nov. 5 as buying interest emerged from Indonesia, though the complex remained saddled with supply amid heavy prompt exports from China.
Indonesia's state-owned Pertamina Patra Niaga sought three 200,000-barrel cargoes for delivery to Jakarta (SKH) over Dec. 17-19 and Dec. 27-29, and loading over Dec. 19-21 from any origin or Straits. The tender closes Nov. 5, with same-day validity.
Meanwhile, Chinese refineries are set to slash overseas shipments of gasoil amid low domestic inventories, but other clean products -- including jet fuel and gasoline -- can be exported as planned, sources said.
According to sources, low inventories of gasoil and gasoline in China have mainly impacted exports of the automotive fuels, but the country's jet fuel outflows are unlikely to face headwinds.
Brokers pegged the balance November-December FOB Singapore jet fuel/kerosene swap time spread -- an indicator of the near-term market outlook -- at plus 35 cents/b in midmorning Asia trading Nov. 5, widening from the Nov. 4 Asian close when Platts assessed the structure flat.

Switzerland has authorized for a second time the release of jet fuel stocks due to temporary shortages, the Swiss Federal Office for National Economic Supply, or FONES, said Nov. 3.
Previously, Switzerland allowed the release of jet fuel stocks between Oct. 15 and Nov. 1 to maintain supplies at Zurich Airport.
In its latest decision, the country has allowed up to 50,000 cubic meters to be drawn until Nov. 29. Including the already authorized draws of up to 30,000 cubic meters, the total volume represents 17.4% of the obligatory jet fuel stocks, FONES said.
US refinery jet fuel yields rose in the week to Oct. 30, according to estimates derived from Energy Information Administration data released Nov. 4. The increase came as distillate regrades favored jet fuel at the Gulf Coast, where export loadings surged.
US jet output rose to a two-month high of 1.969 million b/d last week, the EIA said, which is up by 149,000 b/d from the week prior and higher by 252,000 b/d year over year.
Elsewhere, Taiwan's jet fuel demand rose 23.5% year over year and 26.1% month over month to 20,494 mt in September 2026, data from the Ministry of Economic Affairs' Energy Administration showed.
Taiwan's airlines will raise international flight fuel surcharges from Nov. 8, the Civil Aviation Administration said Nov. 2.
"Due to the recent rise in aviation fuel prices, the passenger fuel surcharge for international routes by national airlines will be increased starting from the 8th of this month, with short-haul routes raised to $40, long-haul routes have been raised to $104," CAA said in the release.
State-owned CPC Corp. said its reference jet A-1 fuel prices for international routes would be raised to $183.09/barrel from $177.89/b, effective Nov. 1.
Since 2005, CAA and domestic airlines have agreed to link surcharges to monthly international jet fuel prices announced by CPC using a tier system, according to the CAA. When the reference price exceeds $50/b, airlines may increase the surcharge for short-haul routes by $2.50 and long-haul routes by $6.50 for every $10/b increase in the price of jet fuel, according to the CAA.
Platts is part of S&P Global Energy. -- Shu ling Lee

However, oil shipments through the Strait of Hormuz are unlikely to fully recover even after a peace deal is reached, as oil companies increase procurement from alternative sources, Takaya Soga, president and CEO of Japan's Nippon Yusen Kaisha, one of the world's largest shipping operators, said Nov. 5.
India imports to EU spike Meanwhile, Europe's jet fuel imports from India spiked to a
one-year high in October amid supply tightness due to missing flows from the Middle East, and strong East-West arbitrage incentives as European prices remain high.
About 310,500 metric tons of jet fuel arrived in Europe from India in October, the highest monthly volumes seen since December 2025, with a further 105,000 mt expected to arrive in November so far, according to Oct. 5 data from S&P Global Commodities at Sea.
Flows from India to Europe had plunged since the EU banned imports of refined oil products made from Russian crude in third countries as part of the EU's 18th sanctions package, which took effect Jan. 21, 2026.
In February 2026, 94,600 mt of Indian jet fuel arrived in Europe, according to CAS, after which flows dried up.
Volumes then gradually began to pick back up after the loss of volume from the Middle East following the US-Israel war with Iran.
European jet market players said that while some market participants remain hesitant to accept barrels from India due to the EU sanctions, the rise in flows can be attributed to very tight supply and stronger East-West arbitrage incentives.
"Although the arbitrage window for jet fuel from India is open, traders still face difficulties placing Indian-origin volumes in Europe. Some buyers are willing to accept the cargoes, while others are not, making the trade a continued risk," a trader said.
A second market player said that some Indian jet fuel volumes arrived in Italy under contract, while others were sold on the spot market to Spain, all accounting for the higher overall volumes. "The main question remains whether buyers will accept Indianorigin volumes," the trader said.
"It was never about the sanctions; it's about the arbs, and now there is just more of an incentive to send more barrels West," a third source said.
Amsterdam-Rotterdam-Antwerp jet fuel and kerosene fuel stocks fell to 3.03 million barrels in October, the lowest level since September 2014, according to Insights Global data.
US jet fuel rose In the US, refinery jet fuel yields rose in the week ended
Oct. 30, according to estimates based on Energy Information Administration data released Nov. 4. The increase came as distillate regrades favored jet fuel at the US Gulf Coast, where export loadings surged.
Jet fuel accounted for 11.80% of US refinery production, up 0.80% from the previous week.

Unseasonably strong demand pushed US distillate inventories to a 23-year low in the week ended Oct. 30, according to EIA data.
On the airline side, Lufthansa Group said in its third interim report Nov. 3 that the number of flights fell 4% year over year to 608,329 during January-September, as business performance was affected by the conflict in the Middle East.
Higher kerosene prices and temporary route cancellations weighed on operating and financial performance, while strong demand on Asian routes supported traffic, according to the report.

Diesel
Chinese refineries halt gasoil exports immediately amid tight supply: sources
Domestic stockpiles hit lowest since 2023
Gas stations in Guangxi stop fuel services
Many Chinese refineries have suspended exports of gasoil to foreign countries with immediate effect to meet domestic demand amid tight supplies, refining sources and traders told Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, Nov. 5.
"We were alerted to prioritize domestic supply amid tight supplies; in other words, we have to suspend gasoil exports to other country to avoid domestic shortages deepening," said a south China-based source with a leading gasoil exporting refinery in the state-run sector.
Six refineries that previously exported to foreign countries now all skip the outflow, despite some continuing to supply Hong Kong and Macau, according to sources at the refineries.
China was estimated to export about 800,000 metric tons of gasoil in November, including the barrels sent to Hong Kong and Macau, amid new export quota allocation, Platts reported earlier.
With the suspension, the volume will fall, except for barrels destined for Hong Kong and Macau, while the resumption will depend on the domestic supply level, the refining sources added.
China's gasoil exports reached 1.85 million mt (459,000 barrels/day) in September, a 43-month high, while October outflows were estimated at about 1.1 million mt.
Eight refiners, including two that focus on gasoline exports, and three local oil product traders said domestic gasoil supply is extremely tight.
China's gasoil stock fell about 6% month over month to 10.48 million mt on Oct. 30, the lowest level since Jan. 6, 2023, when local information provider JLC began providing data.
According to a local trader, the wholesale price of gasoil reached 10,800 yuan/mt ($217/b) on Nov. 3, exceeding the retail price ceiling of 10,025 yuan/mt in Guangzhou, the capital city of Guangdong province, suggesting tightness.
The wholesale price fell to 10,500 yuan/mt on Nov. 5 due to a slump in crude oil prices, the trader said, adding that supply remained tight, supporting the price despite stable demand.

SPAIN DATA: Transport fuel volume rises 2% in October, says Exolum
Volumes rise across all fuel categories
Gasoline recovery boosted by new vehicle sales
Aviation visitor growth to slow in November: ministry
The volume of transport fuel supplied by national distributor Exolum Corporacion SA to the Spanish market rose 2% year over year in October to 3.9 million cubic meters (3.1 million metric tons), with volume rising across all three categories, according to data published by the group on Nov. 4.
The increase was in line with the 2% rise in the first ten months, with 37.3 MMcm distributed during the period.
Exolum's fuel distribution business covers roughly threequarters of total Spanish demand for vehicle fuels and includes biofuel volumes for gasoline and diesel. The company operates 4,000 kilometers of product pipelines and a storage capacity of 8 MMcm in Spain.
In October, gasoline volumes showed the largest increase, rising 5% year over year to 707 MMcm (530,000 mt), the highest October level in Exolum's records. Yearto-date demand for gasoline is at its highest level since 2004, according to historical demand data from the reserve corporation CORES.
The increase has been driven by a general shift toward nonplug-in hybrid vehicles among new auto buyers, which accounted for 39% of auto sales from January to October, with traditional gasoline vehicles at 19% and plug-in hybrids at 12%, according to data from the automobile association ANFAC.
Jet volume delivered to the Spanish retail market was at 782,000 cubic meters (626,000 mt) in October, up 3% year over year and the highest October total on record, as tourism numbers remained bullish. Spain's Tourism Ministry has forecast a 1% year-over-year increase in air visitors in November, following a 6% increase forecast for October.
Diesel volume showed a second consecutive monthly gain in October, rising 1% year over year to 2.44 MMcm (2.07 million mt). Volume in the first 10 months of 2026 was flat at 23.1 MMcm, according to the data.
-- Gianluca Baratti

Platts Asia and Middle Eastern Gasoil Daily Market Analysis
Chinese refineries to halt gasoil exports immediately
Vietnam's Petrolimex seeks 105,000 mt gasoil November-December
Lanka IOC seeks gasoil, gasoline cargo for Dec. 17-18 delivery
The Asian ultra-low sulfur gasoil market saw backwardation supported on Nov. 5, amid talks of suspension of gasoil exports from China.
Brokers pegged the balance-month November-December Singapore gasoil swaps time spread at $1.64/b in midafternoon Asian trading Nov. 5, compared with $1.51/b at the 0830 GMT Asian close Nov. 4.
In the physical segment, the benchmark Platts-assessed cash differential for FOB Singapore gasoil with a maximum sulfur content of 10 parts per million slid 24 cents/b day over day to 44 cents/b at the 0830 GMT Asian close Nov. 4.
Chinese refiners were said to have suspended exports of gasoil to foreign countries with immediate effect to meet domestic demand amid tight supplies, Platts earlier reported.
China was previously estimated to export about 800,000 metric tons of gasoil in November, including barrels sent to Hong Kong and Macau.
China's gasoil stock fell about 6% month over month to 10.48 million mt on Oct. 30, the lowest level since Jan. 6, 2023, when local information provider JLC began providing data.
There was market chatter that some tenders issued by Chinese refiners had been cancelled, though further details were not yet available.
"So far only speculation; no one can confirm," an Asia-based middle distillates trader said.
On tenders, Vietnam's Petrolimex has issued a tender seeking two 35,000 mt cargoes of 500 ppm sulfur gasoil for delivery over Nov. 24-28 and Dec. 4-8, as well as a 35,000 mt cargo of 10 ppm sulfur gasoil for delivery over Nov. 26-30. The tender closes on Nov. 5, with next-day validity.

Meanwhile, Lanka IOC issued a purchase tender seeking a combined cargo comprising 12,000 mt of 500 ppm sulfur gasoil, 2,500 mt of 500 ppm sulfur marine gasoil, 22,000 mt of 92 RON gasoline and 3,500 mt of 95 RON gasoline for delivery over Dec. 17-18 to Colombo. The tender closes on Nov. 5, with next-day validity.
In statistical news, Taiwan's oil products consumption rose 9.6% month over month to 2.3 million metric tons in September but fell 7.3% on a year-over-year basis, led by a decline in naphtha and LPG, data from the Ministry of Economic Affairs' Energy Administration showed Nov. 2.
Taiwan consumed 436,817 mt of gasoil during the month, inching 2.6% higher year over year and largely unchanged month over month.

Platts European Diesel Daily Market Analysis
ICE gasoil futures remain subdued at $1,360.50/mt
US distillate stocks hit lowest since May 2003
The European diesel complex remains subdued on Nov. 5 in the wake of midterm election results, which the market hopes could trigger a de-escalation between the US and Iran.
Platts assessed the front- month ICE low sulfur gasoil futures contract up $5.25/metric ton to $1,360.50/mt on the day, but the price remains well below the October average of $1,442.75/mt.
The relative weakness in the market reflects sentiment- driven movements rather than a change in fundamentals, say traders.
Sustained high diesel cargo and retail pump prices have reduced end- consumer purchasing power, leading to demand destruction across Europe.
Average EU diesel retail prices have held roughly steady over the last month, ranging from 2.136 per liter in the week ended Oct. 12 to 2.154 per liter in the week ended Nov. 2, the latest data from the European Commission published Nov. 5 shows. Comparatively, EU diesel prices averaged 1.012 per liter in the week ended Nov. 1 2025, demonstrating a 113% increase in the price of fuel over the year.
Meanwhile, Europe has seen reduced supply from the US as the US experiences unusually higher demand.
Unseasonably strong demand pushed US distillate stocks to 23-year lows during the week ended Oct. 30, according to US Energy Information Administration data released Nov. 4.
Nationwide distillate stocks declined 3.57 million barrels to 99.26 million barrels during the week, EIA data showed, putting them 12.3% behind the EIA's seasonal five-year average and at the lowest outright level since May 2003.
Total product supplied for distillates -- the EIA's proxy for demand -- climbed 5.5% to 4.44 million barrels/day, putting implied demand 12% above the five-year average and nearly 20% above year-ago levels.

Prioritizing diesel production has been a common theme among refiners in their third-quarter earnings reports.

Middle East bunker demand to improve in Nov amid easing prices; port congestion, delays persist
Fujairah bunker activity improves in Oct
Hormuz vessel transits remain below Oct average
Oman bunker demand remains stable
Middle East bunker demand should improve in November as prices ease, following increased activity at key UAE bunkering hubs in October, four market participants, including traders and suppliers, told Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, Nov. 5.
"In November, during the first week, prices are coming down, and we expect the situation to improve and demand to increase," a Dubai-based trader told Platts Nov. 5.
Low sulfur fuel oil exports from Kuwait have picked up in recent weeks, with the latest cargo of about 40,000 metric tons arriving at the port of Fujairah Nov. 2, according to the traders and data from S&P Global Commodities at Sea.
"In Fujairah, overall, October saw an increase in volume, with an average stem size of 500-600 mt for VLSFO, supported by a good number of inquiries compared to September," the trader said. "Prices are somewhat favorable, and product availability has been consistent throughout the month."
In Khor Fakkan, demand for both HSFO and VLSFO improved, the trader said, with strong product availability throughout the month. Jebel Ali port saw most of the truck supplies, and most of the demand is driven by container vessels, the trader said.
